## Memory

You have access to a memory folder with guidance from prior runs. It can save
time and help you stay consistent. Use it whenever it is likely to help.

Decision boundary: should you use memory for a new user query?

- Skip memory ONLY when the request is clearly self-contained and does not need
  workspace history, conventions, or prior decisions.
- Hard skip examples: current time/date, simple translation, simple sentence
  rewrite, one-line shell command, trivial formatting.
- Use memory by default when ANY of these are true:
  - the query mentions workspace/repo/module/path/files in MEMORY_SUMMARY below,
  - the user asks for prior context / consistency / previous decisions,
  - the task is ambiguous and could depend on earlier project choices,
  - the ask is a non-trivial and related to MEMORY_SUMMARY below.
- If unsure, do a quick memory pass.

Memory layout (general -> specific):

- C:\Users\Joshu\.codex\memories/memory_summary.md (already provided below; do NOT open again)
- C:\Users\Joshu\.codex\memories/MEMORY.md (searchable registry; primary file to query)
- C:\Users\Joshu\.codex\memories/skills/<skill-name>/ (skill folder)
  - SKILL.md (entrypoint instructions)
  - scripts/ (optional helper scripts)
  - examples/ (optional example outputs)
  - templates/ (optional templates)
- C:\Users\Joshu\.codex\memories/rollout_summaries/ (per-rollout recaps + evidence snippets)
  - The paths of these entries can be found in C:\Users\Joshu\.codex\memories/MEMORY.md or C:\Users\Joshu\.codex\memories/rollout_summaries/ as `rollout_path`
  - These files are append-only `jsonl`: `session_meta.payload.id` identifies the session, `turn_context` marks turn boundaries, `event_msg` is the lightweight status stream, and `response_item` contains actual messages, tool calls, and tool outputs.
  - For efficient lookup, prefer matching the filename suffix or `session_meta.payload.id`; avoid broad full-content scans unless needed.

Quick memory pass (when applicable):

1. Skim the MEMORY_SUMMARY below and extract task-relevant keywords.
2. Search C:\Users\Joshu\.codex\memories/MEMORY.md using those keywords.
3. Only if MEMORY.md directly points to rollout summaries/skills, open the 1-2
   most relevant files under C:\Users\Joshu\.codex\memories/rollout_summaries/ or
   C:\Users\Joshu\.codex\memories/skills/.
4. If above are not clear and you need exact commands, error text, or precise evidence, search over `rollout_path` for more evidence.
5. If there are no relevant hits, stop memory lookup and continue normally.

Quick-pass budget:

- Keep memory lookup lightweight: ideally <= 4-6 search steps before main work.
- Avoid broad scans of all rollout summaries.

During execution: if you hit repeated errors, confusing behavior, or suspect
relevant prior context, redo the quick memory pass.

How to decide whether to verify memory:

- Consider both risk of drift and verification effort.
- If a fact is likely to drift and is cheap to verify, verify it before
  answering.
- If a fact is likely to drift but verification is expensive, slow, or
  disruptive, it is acceptable to answer from memory in an interactive turn,
  but you should say that it is memory-derived, note that it may be stale, and
  consider offering to refresh it live.
- If a fact is lower-drift and expensive to verify, it is usually fine to
  answer from memory directly.

When answering from memory without current verification:

- If you rely on memory for a fact that you did not verify in the current turn,
  say so briefly in the final answer.
- If that fact is plausibly drift-prone or comes from an older note, older
  snapshot, or prior run summary, say that it may be stale or outdated.
- If live verification was skipped and a refresh would be useful in the
  interactive context, consider offering to verify or refresh it live.
- Do not present unverified memory-derived facts as confirmed-current.
- Prefer a short refresh offer for interactive questions, especially about prior
  results, commands, timing, or older snapshots.

Memory citation requirements:

- If ANY relevant memory files were used: append exactly one
`<oai-mem-citation>` block as the VERY LAST content of the final reply.
  Normal responses should include the answer first, then append the
`<oai-mem-citation>` block at the end.
- Use this exact structure for programmatic parsing:
```
<oai-mem-citation>
<citation_entries>
MEMORY.md:234-236|note=[responsesapi citation extraction code pointer]
rollout_summaries/2026-02-17T21-23-02-LN3m-example.md:10-12|note=[weekly report format]
</citation_entries>
<rollout_ids>
019c6e27-e55b-73d1-87d8-4e01f1f75043
019c7714-3b77-74d1-9866-e1f484aae2ab
</rollout_ids>
</oai-mem-citation>
```
- `citation_entries` is for rendering:
  - one citation entry per line
  - format: `<file>:<line_start>-<line_end>|note=[<how memory was used>]`
  - use file paths relative to the memory base path (for example, `MEMORY.md`,
    `rollout_summaries/...`, `skills/...`)
  - only cite files actually used under the memory base path (do not cite
    workspace files as memory citations)
  - if you used `MEMORY.md` and then a rollout summary/skill file, cite both
  - list entries in order of importance (most important first)
  - `note` should be short, single-line, and use simple characters only (avoid
    unusual symbols, no newlines)
- `rollout_ids` is for us to track what previous rollouts you find useful:
  - include one rollout id per line
  - rollout ids should look like UUIDs (for example,
    `019c6e27-e55b-73d1-87d8-4e01f1f75043`)
  - include unique ids only; do not repeat ids
  - an empty `<rollout_ids>` section is allowed if no rollout ids are available
  - you can find rollout ids in rollout summary files and MEMORY.md
  - do not include file paths or notes in this section
  - For every `citation_entries`, try to find and cite the corresponding rollout id if possible
- Never include memory citations inside pull-request messages.
- Never cite blank lines; double-check ranges.

Updating memories:

You can update the memories **only** when explicitly asked by the user. This must always come from a direct request from the user.
- Write your update in C:\Users\Joshu\.codex\memories/extensions/ad_hoc/notes/
- Each update must be one small file containing what you want to add/delete/update from the memories.
- The name of this file must be `<timestamp>-<short slug>.md`
- Do not try to edit the memory files yourself, only add one update note in C:\Users\Joshu\.codex\memories/extensions/ad_hoc/notes/

## User Profile

Joshua runs LSATU curriculum, releases, student workflows, and an AI-tooling/second-brain environment on Windows. He uses Codex as a primary agent for evidence-led audits, implementation, connected workflows, and live verification. Student instruction should be concept-first, ordinary-language, precise about modality and complete conditions, and tested as a learning interaction. He is configuring Orca as a multi-agent workspace with Codex primary.

## User preferences

- Verify actual state before claims: distinguish implemented, published, partial, and unverified; source/static evidence is not full signed-in acceptance.
- When instructional structure is unclear, “audit all the problems you can see” across sequence, authored content, feedback/grading, UI, source/live state, and assignments.
- Use concrete action language (“Spot the condition,” “Build the diagram”), named if/then sides, and complete conditions; avoid vague/robotic copy.
- Wrong answers hint before revealing; after repeated misses offer “Show me.” Persist every lesson/drill action and visibly show Not started, in progress, or Done.
- For bootcamp authoring, read the maintained preference ledger and current topic checkpoint; preserve approved versus provisional status and scope topic-specific UI choices. [ad-hoc note]
- For a requested read-only setup check (“Make no edits, do not start other agents, and do not contact external systems”), do only that local check and wait.
- Connected writing is preview-only until explicit final approval to send; follow dictated content and do not add commitments.
- Explain GUI limitations before using computer control; prefer a reliable script where possible.

## General Tips

- Read C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\AGENTS.md before AI OS work: wiki/hot.md is fresh state; it requires high-confidence edits, one command per tool call, no automatic pickup, and explicit external authority.
- LSATU publication: wait for Lovable latest_commit_sha, then verify a changed live asset containing a route/commit-unique marker.
- Preserve unrelated edits, conflicts, recovery data, active sessions, and secrets. Never retain exposed keys.
- Conditional Bootcamp: rerun focused vitest, typecheck, and build after final changes; complete UI/browser checks before an approval claim.
- Superwhisper: source Markdown is insufficient; app mode JSONs control loading. Run sync.py --restart and read live settings/counts. Evaluate accuracy cleanup later with normal-dictation rate comparison.
- Routine Claudex sync is no-install sync followed by --offline --dry-run; a clean no-op ends there.

## developer

<multi_agent_role>You are `/root`, the primary agent in a team of agents collaborating to fulfill the user's goals.

At the start of your turn, you are the active agent.
You can spawn sub-agents to handle subtasks, and those sub-agents can spawn their own sub-agents.
All agents in the team, including the agents that you can assign tasks to, are equally intelligent and capable, and have access to the same set of tools.

You can use `spawn_agent` to create a new agent, `followup_task` to give an existing agent a new task and trigger a turn, and `send_message` to pass a message to a running agent without triggering a turn.
`send_message` calls may be read by a human, so ensure they are legible. Always put proper spaces between words and/or numbers.
Child agents can also spawn their own sub-agents.
You can decide how much context you want to propagate to your sub-agents with the `fork_turns` parameter.

You will receive messages in the analysis channel in the form:
```
Message Type: MESSAGE | FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: <recipient>
Sender: <author>
Payload:
<payload text>
```
They may be addressed as to=/root

Note that collaboration tools cannot be called from inside `functions.exec`. Call `spawn_agent`, `send_message`, `followup_task`, `wait_agent`, `interrupt_agent`, and `list_agents` only as direct tool calls using the recipient shown in their tool definitions, such as `to=functions.collaboration.spawn_agent`, since they are intentionally absent from the `functions.exec` `tools.*` namespace. Available tools in `functions.exec` are explicitly described with a `tools` namespace in the developer message.

All agents share the same directory. In detail:
- All agents have access to the same container and filesystem as you.
- All agents use the same current working directory.
- As a result, edits made by one agent are immediately visible to all other agents.

When calling `wait_agent`, prefer longer waits (minutes) to avoid busy polling.

There are 4 available concurrency slots, meaning that up to 4 agents can be active at once, including you.

Full-history forks (`fork_turns` omitted or `"all"`) inherit the parent model and reasoning effort and do not accept overrides. Only set `model` or `reasoning_effort` when explicitly requested by the user, applicable `AGENTS.md` instructions, or skill instructions; when doing so, set `fork_turns` to `"none"`.</multi_agent_role>

# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os-projects\lsatu-four-situations

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: target 34 one-to-one sessions a week
plus consultations (calendar showed ~13/week, 2026-07 to 2026-10). Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

# AGENTS.md

This file is the Codex-facing adapter for this repository. It summarizes the Claude-oriented project guidance from `CLAUDE.md` in a smaller format that is fast for Codex to scan. `CLAUDE.md` remains the fuller source of project knowledge.

## Fast Locator

- Vault root (the `~/ai-os` directory IS the Obsidian vault): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os`
- Code repo root (this project): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/projects/lsatu`
- Live vault knowledge: `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/wiki/` (hubs, voice, business), `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/students/`, `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/corpus/`
- Pre-2026-07-16 LSATU product knowledge (archived, read-only): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/archive/wiki-pre-rebuild-2026-07-16/domains/lsat-u/`

If an older note says `~/Documents/Obsidian Vault/...` or `~/Documents/Claude/02_PROJECTS/LSATU`, treat it as stale — the vault was consolidated into `~/ai-os`. Use the absolute paths above.

## Project Map

- `src/App.tsx`: route registration and top-level app wiring.
- `src/pages/`: route-level screens, including the Foyer and admin/teacher surfaces.
- `src/components/`: shared UI, including `foyer/`, `inbox/`, and shadcn/ui primitives.
- `src/hooks/`: auth, permissions, and shared data hooks.
- `src/lib/`: drill, WAJ, gamification, and other product logic.
- `src/integrations/supabase/`: generated Supabase client and types. Do not hand-edit generated types.
- `supabase/functions/`: Edge Functions.
- `supabase/migrations/`: database schema migrations.

## Stack And Commands

- Stack: React 18, Vite 5, TypeScript, Tailwind, shadcn/ui, Supabase, TanStack Query.
- Package manager: `npm`. `package-lock.json` is canonical. Ignore stray Bun lockfiles.
- Dev server: `npm run dev` on port `8080`.
- Build: `npm run build`
- Lint: `npm run lint`
- Test: `npm run test`
- Watch tests: `npm run test:watch`
- Typecheck: `npm run typecheck`. Required before a push — `vite build` strips types without checking them, so a type error would ship. `.claude/safe-push.sh` gates on it.
- E2E: `npm run test:e2e` (Playwright, `e2e/smoke.spec.ts`).

## Working Rules

- Keep changes small and aligned with existing patterns.
- Use TypeScript React function components and the `@/` path alias.
- Preserve Lovable-specific plumbing, including the `lovable-tagger` Vite plugin, the PWA setup, and the service-worker guard in `src/main.tsx`.
- Do not change app behavior just to satisfy tooling setup.
- Do not hardcode secrets or service-role credentials.
- Treat Supabase-generated files as generated artifacts, not hand-edited source.
- TypeScript strictness is intentionally loose. Do not tighten it without explicit approval.
- Keep this adapter aligned with `CLAUDE.md` without duplicating the full project guide. Put durable detail in `CLAUDE.md`; keep shared reference material in `docs/`, `references/`, or `templates/`.

## Product Constraints

- The Foyer is the primary navigation surface. The `GooeyDock` quick-launch row belongs only on `/foyer`.
- Inbox is a real 1:1 student-admin messaging system with PDF attachments.
- Admin surfaces must stay responsive on desktop and mobile.
- Prefer truthful fallbacks over fake buttons, fake analytics, or placeholder behavior.
- Onboarding asks for name, not username. Sign-in uses email. The instructor display name is `Joshua`.

## Smart Drill Note

On `/drill`, adaptive Smart Drill can show a `currentQuestion` that is not the same as `session.questionQueue[session.currentIndex]`. Any per-question UI state must key off `currentQuestion.qid` unless the code path is explicitly limited to queued drills.

## Verification (must not skip)

- Any change to `src/components/` or `src/pages/` is not "done" until it has been viewed running in the app (dev server + a browser tool), with a screenshot or DOM check as evidence. Never report a UI change complete on the strength of the diff alone. This is the single biggest recurring failure across past sessions — a hard gate, not a suggestion.
- **Responsive fixes carry a wider gate: verify across the full emulate matrix BEFORE pushing, not after Joshua reports it.** Narrow, mid, and wide viewports plus the sidecar-docked case, every time. A pushed "fix" that does not hold costs a second full session; this has already happened once.

## Workflow Expectations

- Run `npm run build` before wrapping up significant code or documentation work.
- If tests are relevant to the change, run them too.
- After completing a change, stage the intended files, commit with a concise message, and push to `origin main` unless the user says otherwise.
- **Push via `.claude/safe-push.sh`, not raw `git push`.** Lovable auto-commits to `main` mid-session, so a raw push can be rejected or race a concurrent commit. The script fetches, merges `origin/main` if it diverged (never rebases, never force-pushes), then pushes. It also gates on `npm run typecheck`.
- **Never roll `main` back.** Do not use Lovable's "Revert to commit", "revert and resend", or history restore, and never reset or force-push `main`. On 2026-09-27 an accidental "revert and resend" (b998596) silently undid three merged PRs across 121 question-bank files, and students saw the wrong questions again. To undo your own work, `git revert <your SHA>` only. If a Lovable dialog offers a revert, dismiss it; if one fires, stop and tell Joshua before repairing anything.
- Existing Codex repo hooks live in `.codex/hooks.json`; preserve them unless the task explicitly requires a hook change.

## Session Protocol (second brain)

Product context, status, and decisions live in the Obsidian vault at `~/ai-os/`, not this repo. Two repo-local skills in `.agents/skills/` run the session ritual; the canonical protocol is in the vault `CLAUDE.md`.

The vault was rebuilt 2026-07-16 (paths relative to `~/ai-os`). Live: teaching voice `wiki/teaching-voice.md` (route ALL student-facing copy through it), the business `wiki/entities/aspiring-attorneys.md`, a student's record `students/<name>/` (see `students/README.md`), teaching knowledge `corpus/index.md`, and the `wiki/{tasks,hot,dashboard,index,log}.md` hubs named below. Archived read-only reference (pre-rebuild product knowledge, may be stale): `archive/wiki-pre-rebuild-2026-07-16/domains/lsat-u/` — product context `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-product.md`, design system `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-design.md`, quality bar `…/synthesis/bootcamp-quality-bar.md`, domain index `…/index.md`.

- `pickup` — start of session. Read `wiki/tasks.md` (the Now / Next / Done ledger) first, then `wiki/hot.md`, `wiki/dashboard.md`, skim `wiki/index.md`, read the latest `wiki/log.md` entry. Emit a 6-8 line briefing (where we left off, Now, Next, open threads, hot, suggested next) and wait. Do not deep-read other pages until pointed at a task.
- `wrapup` — end of session. Propose which `## Now` tasks finished and confirm before writing; a wrong "Done" is the failure to avoid. Then strike finished items through in `wiki/tasks.md` with a date, refresh `wiki/hot.md`, append a dated `wiki/log.md` entry (newest first, distinct label for same-day sessions), update the task anchor.

`wiki/tasks.md` is the single source of truth for what is open vs done. Finished work is struck through so a new session never re-runs it. `raw/` in the vault is immutable.

## Codex Adapter Layout

- `.codex/config.toml`: minimal shared Codex project config. Keep secrets and machine-specific overrides out of this file.
- `.codex/agents/`: Codex agent definitions converted from `.claude/agents/*.md` when Claude agents exist. Use `.toml` files with converted instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- `.agents/skills/`: repository-local Codex skills copied from `.claude/skills/` when Claude skills exist and Codex should use them. Keep each `SKILL.md` with its supporting files.
- `.codex/hooks.json`: existing repository hooks. Preserve unless the task explicitly concerns hooks.

This checkout currently has Claude settings and hooks under `.claude/`, but no `.claude/skills/` or `.claude/agents/` directory to convert.

## Known Gaps

- Vitest specs exist (80 `src/**/*.test.ts(x)` files as of 2026-07-16); run with `npm run test`. Playwright is wired with one e2e spec (`e2e/smoke.spec.ts`), run via `npm run test:e2e`.
- No Prettier config and no CI workflows.
- `README.md` is still minimal.

## Agent skills

### Issue tracker

GitHub Issues on `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db`, via the `gh` CLI. See `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.

### Triage labels

The five canonical roles, unchanged: `needs-triage`, `needs-info`, `ready-for-agent`, `ready-for-human`, `wontfix`. See `docs/agents/triage-labels.md`.

### Domain docs

Multi-context: `CONTEXT-MAP.md` points at eight per-context glossaries; ADRs are all at the root. See `docs/agents/domain.md`.

Relative paths depend on that device\'s current working directory, and tilde paths (~/...) may not resolve in every context. Absolute paths are generally the most reliable.\\n                        This command can be referenced as \\"DC: ...\\" or \\"use Desktop Commander to ...\\" in your instructions. This tool is part of plugin `Remote Desktop Commander`.\\n\\nexec tool declaration:\\n```ts\\ndeclare const tools: { mcp__codex_apps__remote_desktop_commander_read_multiple_files(args: {\\n  // The ID of the device to run this tool on (from list_devices). Windows devices commonly use PowerShell or CMD; macOS and Linux devices commonly use Unix shells. Use commands and paths appropriate for the selected device.\\n                        \\n                        Examples:\\n                        • start_process(\\"python3 -i\\") → Start an interactive Python REPL\\n                        • start_process(\\"node -i\\") → Start an interactive Node.js REPL\\n                        • start_process(\\"node:local\\") → Run stateless Node.js code on the MCP server\\n                        • start_process(\\"wc -l /path/file.csv\\") → Run a one-shot shell command\\n\\n                        Interactive process sessions support follow-up input after they are started.\\n                        Long-running process sessions expose newly available output while they remain active.\\n                        Binary files can be processed with appropriate command-line tools or libraries available\\n                        in the selected process environment.\\n\\n                        SMART DETECTION:\\n                        - Detects REPL prompts (>>>, >, $, etc.)\\n                        - Identifies when a process is waiting for input\\n                        - Recognizes process completion vs timeout\\n                        - Early exit prevents unnecessary waiting\\n                        \\n                        STATES DETECTED:\\n                        Process waiting for input (shows prompt)\\n                        Process finished execution\\n                        Process running (use read_process_output)\\n\\n                        PERFORMANCE DEBUGGING (verbose_timing parameter):\\n                        Set verbose_timing: true to get detailed timing information including:\\n                        - Exit reason (early_exit_quick_pattern, early_exit_periodic_check, process_exit, timeout)\\n                        - Total duration and time to first output\\n                        - Complete timeline of all output events with timestamps\\n                        - Which detection mechanism triggered early exit\\n\\n                        Paths refer to the selected device. Relative paths depend on that device\'s current working directory, and tilde paths (~/...) may not resolve in every context. Absolute paths are generally the most reliable.\\n                        This command can be referenced as \\"DC: ...\\" or \\"use Desktop Commander to ...\\" in your instructions.\\n\\nREMOTE EXECUTION:\\nRemote execution waits at most 3 seconds for completion or a prompt. If the process is still running, it continues in the background and returns its PID. Larger timeout_ms values are accepted but capped at 3000ms. This tool is part of plugin `Remote Desktop Commander`.\\n\\nexec tool declaration:\\n```ts\\ndeclare const tools: { mcp__codex_apps__remote_desktop_commander_start_process(args: {\\n  command: string;\\n  // The ID of the device to run this tool on (from list_devices). Required when more than one device is online; may be omitted when only one device is connected.\\n  deviceId?: string;\\n  shell?: string;\\n  timeout_ms?: number;\\n  verbose_timing?: boolean;\\n}): Promise<CallToolResult>; };\\n```"}'}]

Use `nodeRepl.write(value)` for output and `await nodeRepl.emitImage(image)` for images. Execution context is available through `nodeRepl.cwd`, `nodeRepl.homeDir`, `nodeRepl.tmpDir`, and `nodeRepl.requestMeta`. Use `js_add_node_module_dir` when an additional package directory is required.\\n\\nexec tool declaration:\\n```ts\\ndeclare const tools: { mcp__node_repl__js(args: {\\n  // JavaScript code to execute with top-level await.\\n  code: string;\\n  // Optional execution timeout in milliseconds. The rule is not about all gullies everywhere."\n\nsource-independent-31\nstatement: "When a mix of fresh and dried grass clippings is worked into garden soil, that soil holds water longer."\nindicators: [{"text":"When","group":1}]\nsufficient: "a mix of fresh and dried grass clippings is worked into garden soil"\nnecessary: "the soil holds water longer"\nifThen: "If a mix of fresh and dried grass clippings is worked into garden soil, then that soil holds water longer."\ncontrapositiveWords: "If garden soil does not hold water longer, then a mix of fresh and dried grass clippings was not worked into that soil."\nexplanation: "Keep both fresh and dried clippings in the condition. i < remaining.length; i++) for (let j = 0; j < remaining.length; j++) if (i !== j && `${remaining[i].text} ${remaining[j].text}` === text) pairs.push([i, j]);\n3777:     const pair = pairs[0];\n3778:     if (pair) {\n3779:       if (remaining[pair[0]].group !== selection.group || remaining[pair[1]].group !== selection.group) wrongGroup.push(selection.text);\n3780:       for (const i of [...pair].sort((a, b) => b - a)) remaining.splice(i, 1);\n3781:       continue;\n3782:     }\n3783:     if (whoIntroducesCondition && WHO_PHRASE.test(text)) {\n3784:       if (selection.group !== \'1\') wrongGroup.push(selection.text);\n3785:       continue;\n3786:     }\n3787:     extra.push(selection.text);\n3788:   }\n3789:   if (extra.length) {\n3790:     const text = extra[0];\n3791:     const normal = normalizeIndicator(text);\n3792:     if (expected.some(e => e.text !== normal && containsRun(e.text, normal))) return { correct: false, hint: `“${text}” is only part of an indicator. Select every word of the phrase.` };\n3793:     if (expected.some(e => containsRun(normal, e.text))) return { correct: false, hint: `“${text}” includes words that are not part of the indicator. Select only the signal words.` };\n3794:     return { correct: false, hint: `“${text}” is not an indicator in this rule.` };\n3795:   }\n3796:   if (wrongGroup.length) return { correct: false, hint: `Check the group for “${wrongGroup[0]}”. Does it introduce what is enough, or what is required?` };\n3797:   const missing = remaining.filter(e => !e.optional);\n3798:   if (missing.length) return { correct: false, hint: missing.length === 1 ? \'One indicator is still unselected. Read the whole rule again.\' : `${missing.length} indicators are still unselected. Read the whole rule again.` };\n3799:   return { correct: true, hint: \'\' };\n3800: }'}]

That is all it tells you.</strong></p>\n-      <p>Take this rule: <strong>If the pool is open, then a lifeguard is on duty.</strong></p>\n-      <p>The original: if the pool is open, a lifeguard must be on duty. The contrapositive: <strong>if no lifeguard is on duty, the pool can’t be open.</strong> The rule says nothing else.</p>\n+    <Section title="When a rule settles something">\n+      <p className="ct-definition"><strong>A rule settles something in two situations: when the sufficient condition is present, and when the necessary condition is absent.</strong></p>\n+      <p>Take this rule: <strong>If the pool is open, then a lifeguard must be on duty.</strong></p>\n+      <p>The pool is open? Then a lifeguard must be on duty. That’s the original rule. No lifeguard on duty? Then <strong>the pool can’t be open.</strong> That’s the contrapositive. In the other two situations, the rule settles nothing.</p>\n     </Section>\n     <Section title="Don’t fall for the necessary condition">\n-      <p>The LSAT will give you a rule, then tell you the necessary condition is present. A lifeguard is on duty! You’ll want to conclude the pool is open. Don’t. The lifeguard could be there for a training session with the pool closed.</p>\n+      <p>When the necessary condition is present, <PulseWord>the rule settles nothing.</PulseWord> The pool could be open or closed.</p>\n     </Section>\n     <Section title="Four situations">\n-      <p>Any fact about a rule puts you in one of four situations. Two of them settle something. Two settle nothing.</p>\n+      <p>Any fact about the rule puts you in one of four situations.</p>\n       <SituationChart/>\n     </Section>\n     <Section title="Ask where the fact lands">\n-      <p>When a rule comes with a fact, check which side the fact is about. Sufficient present or necessary absent, and the rule settles something. Sufficient absent or necessary present, and it settles nothing, so look for help somewhere else in the argument.</p>\n+      <p>When a rule comes with a fact, check which side the fact is about before you conclude anything. If the fact lands in one of the two situations that settle nothing, look for help somewhere else in the argument.</p>\n     </Section>\n   </>;\n }\ndiff --git a/src/bootcamps/conditionals/components/MixedFamilyPractice.tsx b/src/bootcamps/conditionals/components/MixedFamilyPractice.tsx\nindex 0c30c832..6b8d48c3 100644\n--- a/src/bootcamps/conditionals/components/MixedFamilyPractice.tsx\n+++ b/src/bootcamps/conditionals/components/MixedFamilyPractice.tsx\n@@ -24,7 +24,7 @@ type MixedItem = {\n export const mixedFamilyItems: MixedItem[] = [\n   {\n     sentence: \'The match moves indoors if the forecast shows lightning.\', word: \'if\', family: \'sufficient\',\n-    targetPrompt: \'What does the forecast have to show for the match to move indoors under this rule?\', targetFeedback: \'You identified the forecast showing lightning.\',\n+    targetPrompt: \'What does the rule say is enough to move the match indoors?\', targetFeedback: \'You identified the forecast showing lightning.\',\n     target: \'the forecast shows lightning\', partial: \'the forecast\', other: \'the match moves indoors\',\n     left: \'Forecast shows lightning\', right: \'Match moves indoors\',\n     partialFeedback: "The forecast alone isn\'t something that can be true or false. Anything that no longer matches the current question set starts fresh. */\n125: function restoreMixed(state: unknown): MixedState | null {\n126:   const data = state as { questionIds?: unknown; current?: unknown; visited?: unknown; results?: unknown; finished?: unknown; introducedStages?: unknown } | undefined;\n127:   if (!data || !Array.isArray(data.questionIds) || !data.results || typeof data.results !== \'object\') return null;\n128:   const ids = data.questionIds as unknown[];\n129:   if (ids.length < baselineQuestions.length || baselineQuestions.some((question, index) => ids[index] !== question.id)) return null;\n130:   const extra = ids.slice(baselineQuestions.length).map(id => confirmationPool.find(item => item.id === id));\n131:   if (extra.some(item => !item)) return null;\n132:   const questions: Question[] = [...baselineQuestions, ...extra.map(item => ({ id: item!.id, stage: 5, kind: \'choice\' as const, item: item! }))];\n133:   const results: Record<string, Result> = {};\n134:   for (const [id, raw] of Object.entries(data.results as Record<string, Partial<Result>>)) {\n135:     if (raw && typeof raw.firstCorrect === \'boolean\' && typeof raw.correct === \'boolean\' && Array.isArray(raw.errors)) results[id] = { firstCorrect: raw.firstCorrect, correct: raw.correct, errors: raw.errors.filter((error): error is MixedDrillError => error in errorLabels) };\n136:   }\n137:   const current = Number.isInteger(data.current) && (data.current as number) >= 0 && (data.current as number) < questions.length ? data.current as number : 0;\n138:   const visited = Array.isArray(data.visited) ? (data.visited as unknown[]).filter((id): id is string => questions.some(question => question.id === id)) : [];\n139:   const introducedStages = Array.isArray(data.introducedStages) ? (data.introducedStages as unknown[]).filter((value): value is number => Number.isInteger(value)) : [];\n140:   return { questions, current, visited: visited.length ? visited : [questions[current].id], results, finished: data.finished === true, introducedStages };\n141: }\n142: \n143: export function OpeningMixedDrill({ stepId }: { stepId?: string } = {}) {\n144:   const course = useCourseStep(stepId);\n145:   const saveStep = course?.record;\n146:   const savedState = course?.saved?.state;\n147:   const [restored] = useState(() => restoreMixed(savedState));\n148:   const [questions, setQuestions] = useState<Question[]>(restored?.questions ?? baselineQuestions);\n149:   const [current, setCurrent] = useState(restored?.current ?? 0);\n150:   const [visited, setVisited] = useState(restored?.visited ?? []);\n154:   // The account can finish loading after the drill opens. { ...previous, [id]: { ...old, correct: false } } : previous;\n188:       return { ...previous, [id]: old ? { ...old, correct, errors: [...new Set([...old.errors, ...errors])] } : { firstCorrect: correct, correct, errors } };\n189:     });\n190:   }, []);\n191:   function move(index: number) {\n192:     touched.current = true;\n193:     setCurrent(index);\n194:     setVisited(previous => previous.includes(questions[index].id) ? previous : [...previous, questions[index].id]);\n195:   }\n196:   const pendingConfirmations = questions.length === baselineQuestions.length ? confirmationQuestions(results) : [];\n197:   function next() {\n198:     touched.current = true;\n199:     if (current + 1 < questions.length) return move(current + 1);\n200:     if (questions.length === baselineQuestions.length) {\n201:       const confirmations = pendingConfirmations;\n202:       if (confirmations.length) {\n203:         setQuestions(previous => [...previous, ...confirmations]);\n204:         setVisited(previous => [...previous, confirmations[0].id]);\n205:         setCurrent(current + 1);\n206:         return;\n207:       }\n208:     }\n209:     setFinished(true);\n210:   }\n211: \n212:   const firstCorrect = baselineQuestions.filter(item => results[item.id]?.firstCorrect).length;\n213:   const corrected = baselineQuestions.filter(item => results[item.id]?.firstCorrect === false && results[item.id]?.correct).length;\n214:   const confirmations = questions.filter(item => item.stage === 5);\n215:   return <section ref={runner} className="omd-runner" aria-label="Conditional translation practice">\n216:     <div className="omd-summary" hidden={!finished}><h2 ref={finished ? heading : undefined} tabIndex={-1}>Review your practice</h2>\n217:       <p>You answered {firstCorrect} of {baselineQuestions.length} correctly on your first try. You corrected {corrected} after checking the explanation.</p>\n218:       <p>This result covers your answer choices and the diagrams you built with the supplied words. Your written translations are for you to compare with the examples.</p>\n219:       {!!confirmations.length && <><h3>How the new sentences went</h3><ul>{confirmations.map(item => <li key={item.id}>{item.id === obligationConfirmationItem.id ? \'What is required and what actually happened\' : item.kind === \'choice\' && errorLabels[item.item.skill]}: {results[item.id]?.firstCorrect ? \'you answered the new question correctly on your first try\' : \'you corrected the new question after checking the explanation. heading : undefined} tabIndex={-1}>{stage.title}</h2>\n225:       <p>{stage.purpose}</p>\n226:       {stage.example && <blockquote>{stage.example}</blockquote>}\n227:       <p>{stage.explanation}</p><p>{stage.instruction}</p>\n228:       <button type="button" className="omd-primary" onClick={() => { touched.current = true; setIntroducedStages(previous => [...previous, question.stage]); }}>{question.stage === 0 ? \'Begin practice\' : \'Continue\'}</button>\n229:     </div>\n230:     <div hidden={finished || showIntroduction}>\n231:     <div className="omd-progress-heading"><p>{question.stage < 5 ? `Stage ${question.stage + 1} of 5` : `${baselineQuestions.length} main questions completed`}</p><p>{question.stage < 5 ? baselineQuestions.length : questions.length - baselineQuestions.length) * 100}%` }}/></div>\n233:     <h2 ref={!finished && !showIntroduction ? heading : undefined} tabIndex={-1}>{stage.title}</h2><p className="omd-purpose">{stage.purpose}</p>\n234:     </div>\n235:     {questions.filter(item => visited.includes(item.id)).map(item => <div key={item.id} hidden={finished || showIntroduction || item.id !== question.id} data-mixed-question={item.id}><RetainedQuestion question={item} record={record}/></div>)}\n236:     {!finished && !showIntroduction && <nav className="omd-navigation" aria-label="Practice questions"><button type="button" disabled={current === 0} onClick={() => move(current - 1)}>Previous question</button><button type="button" className="omd-primary" disabled={!results[question.id]?.correct} onClick={next}>{current === questions.length - 1 ? [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 0.7 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '1: import { ContrapositiveLecture, DoubleNegativeDemo, FlipAndNegateDemo, Neg, PopQuizTitle, PulseWord, PunchWord, RuleDrill, ThreeMoves } from \'./ContrapositiveVisuals\';\n2: import { Children, cloneElement, Fragment, isValidElement, useEffect, useId, useRef, useState, type ReactElement, type ReactNode } from \'react\';\n3: import { Fundamentals, GroupLessons } from \'./ConditionalOpeningLesson\';\n4: import { conditionalReferenceContent, meaningRule, ReferenceQuestion, RuleDiagram, TargetReader, targetExamples, WordFamily } from \'./ConditionalReferenceSequence\';\n5: import { StagedReasoningDrill } from \'./StagedReasoningDrill\';\n6: import { FourSituationsNote, FourSituationsQuiz } from \'./FourSituations\';\n7: import { NecessaryNote } from \'./NecessaryNote\';\n8: import { DrillOneExperience, type DrillOneQuestion } from \'./DrillOneExperience\';\n9: import \'./must-arrow-note.css\';\n10: import { MustArrowPractice } from \'./MustArrowPractice\';\n11: \n12: type ContentSection = ReactElement<{ id?: string; number?: number; title?: string; children?: ReactNode; dark?: boolean; \'data-reference-boundary\'?: boolean }>;\n13: \n14: // Compose the approved source sections directly. Their stateful exercises are\n15: // mounted by React; the former reader-page renderer is never invoked here.\n16: function sections(children: ReactNode): ContentSection[] {\n17:   return Children.toArray(children).flatMap(child => {\n18:     if (!isValidElement<ContentSection[\'props\']>(child)) return [];\n19:     return child.type === Fragment ? sections(child.props.children) : [child];\n20:   });\n21: }\n22: \n23: function reference(number: number): ContentSection {\n24:   const section = sections(conditionalReferenceContent().props.children).find(item => item.props.number === number);\n25:   if (!section) throw new Error(`Missing approved conditional reference section ${number}`);\n26:   return section;\n27: }\n28: \n29: function groupSections(group: \'group-1\' | \'group-2\') {\n30:   return sections(GroupLessons({ id: group }).props.children);\n31: }\n32: \n33: function groupSection(group: \'group-1\' | \'group-2\', title: string) {\n34:   const section = groupSections(group).find(item => item.props.id === title || item.props.title === title);\n35:   if (!section) throw new Error(`Missing approved conditional section: ${title}`);\n36:   const sectionId = `course-${group}-${title.toLowerCase().replace(/[^a-z0-9]+/g, \'-\')}`;\n37:   return cloneElement(section, { key: sectionId, id: sectionId });\n38: }\n39: \n40: function NoteSection({ title, children }: { title: string; children: ReactNode }) {\n41:   return <section className="co-section"><div className="co-section-heading"><h2>{title}</h2></div>{children}</section>;\n42: }\n43: \n44: function ReferenceBody({ number, omitTransition = false }: { number: number; omitTransition?: boolean }) {\n45:   const source = reference(number);\n46:   const children = Children.toArray(source.props.children);\n47:   // These source sections end in navigation prose for the former ordering.\n48:   // The course shell now owns transitions; the teaching stays in its source.\n49:   return <section className={`co-section cr-course-section ${source.props.dark ? \'cr-dark\' : \'\'}`} data-reference-source={number}>\n50:     <div className="co-section-heading"><h2>{source.props.title}</h2></div>\n51:     <div className="cr-step-body">{omitTransition ? children.slice(0, -1) : children}</div>\n52:   </section>;\n53: }\n54: \n55: function FamilySupplement({ group }: { group: \'group-1\' | \'group-2\' }) {\n56:   const source = reference(group === \'group-1\' ? 3 : 5);\n57:   const family = sections(source.props.children).find(child => child.type === WordFamily);\n58:   if (!family) throw new Error(`Missing approved ${group} supplementary family`);\n59:   return <details className="co-practical-note"><summary>More indicators for the same rule</summary>\n60:     <p>Use the stated rule: anyone who borrows a locker has a key. These rules keep that connection. They do not say every person with a key borrows a locker.</p>\n61:     {family}\n62:   </details>;\n63: }\n64: \n65: // Only and only if both mark the requirement, from different places in the rule.\n66: function OnlyAndOnlyIf() {\n67:   return <NoteSection title="Only and only if can mark the same requirement">\n68:     <div className="cr-language-pair"><p><strong>Only</strong> [people with a key] borrow a locker.</p><p>A person borrows a locker <strong>only if</strong> [they have a key].</p><span>Only and only if identify the necessary condition here.</span></div>\n69:     <p>Both sentences say locker borrowers have a key. In the first, only is at the beginning. In the second, only if is in the middle. Having a key is required in both, so it goes right. Read only if as the whole phrase; the if inside it isn\'t doing the job if does on its own.</p>\n70:     <ReferenceQuestion prompt="A person borrows a locker only if they have a key. Which condition goes on the right?" options={[\'Borrows a locker\', \'Has a key\']} correct={1} feedback={[\'Borrowing is what has the requirement. Ask what a locker borrower must have: a key. Borrowing goes left; having a key goes right.\', \'Having a key is required for borrowing. Only if appears in the middle, but it still identifies the necessary condition.\']}/>\n71:   </NoteSection>;\n72: }\n73: \n74: // Only after is tested in Drills 2 and 5, so it is taught with the other Group 2 words.\n75: function OnlyAfter() {\n76:   return <NoteSection title="Only after also marks a requirement">\n77:     <blockquote>The pharmacy fills a prescription <strong>only after</strong> a doctor signs it.</blockquote>\n78:     <p>Use the What Test: ask what the indicator refers to. <strong>Only after what?</strong> A doctor signs it. That signature is required before the pharmacy fills the prescription, so it goes right.</p>\n79:     <RuleDiagram left="Pharmacy fills the prescription" right="A doctor has signed it"/>\n80:     <p>Read it as: if the pharmacy fills a prescription, a doctor has signed it. The word after tells you the order of events in the rule. The arrow still reads as must, not as a timeline.</p>\n81:     <ReferenceQuestion prompt="A doctor has signed Ana\'s prescription. Does this rule prove the pharmacy fills it?" options={[\'Yes. The signature is enough.\', \'No. Whether it gets filled is still open.\']} correct={1} feedback={[\'The signature is what filling requires. Could a doctor sign a prescription that Ana never brings to the pharmacy? The rule would allow that.\', \'The signature is required, not enough. The rule tells you what every filled prescription has. It does not say every signed one gets filled.\']}/>\n82:   </NoteSection>;\n83: }\n84: \n85: // Follows the Group 1 indicator note, whose if, all and any sections already\n86: // teach the report, registered-guest and late-applicant sentences.\n87: function CompleteConditionNote() {\n88:   return <NoteSection title="Keep the whole phrase before you place it">\n89:     <blockquote><strong>The only people admitted</strong> are members.</blockquote>\n90:     <p>The only what? People admitted. An admitted person must be a member, so <strong>Person admitted → Person is a member</strong>. Keep the whole phrase together. The rule doesn\'t say every member gets in.</p>\n91:     <ReferenceQuestion prompt="The only notebooks kept on this shelf are hardback. Which condition is sufficient?" options={[\'Notebook is hardback\', \'Notebook is kept on this shelf\', \'Notebook\']} correct={1} feedback={[\'Being hardback does not guarantee a place on this shelf. The rule tells us about the notebooks kept here.\', \'A notebook kept on this shelf must be hardback. Kept on this shelf is the complete sufficient condition.\', \'Notebook alone would cover every notebook. Keep the restriction: kept on this shelf.\']}/>\n92:     <p>In the next drill, find the indicator, select the complete condition, place it beside the arrow and check that your diagram keeps the rule\'s meaning.</p>\n93:   </NoteSection>;\n94: }\n95: \n96: function FourRules() {\n97:   return <section className="co-section"><div className="co-section-heading"><PopQuizTitle/></div><RuleDrill/></section>;\n98: }\n99: \n100: function ContrapositiveNote() {\n101:   return <>\n102:     <NoteSection title="What a contrapositive is">\n103:       <p>If the sufficient condition happens, the necessary condition <strong>must</strong> happen. The left side of the arrow guarantees the right side.</p>\n104:       <p>The contrapositive is what you know when the right side of the arrow goes missing:</p>\n105:       <p className="ct-definition"><strong>If the <span className="ct-hl">necessary condition</span> is absent, the <span className="ct-hl" data-tone="amber">sufficient condition</span> is absent too.</strong></p>\n106:       <p>Take this rule: <strong>If the ceiling light is on, then the power must be on.</strong> With no power, the light can\'t be on. No power, no light. As a rule: <strong>If the power is off, then the ceiling light must be off.</strong></p>\n107:     </NoteSection>\n108:     <NoteSection title="You already knew this">\n109:       <p>The contrapositive was inside the original rule the whole time, waiting for you to spot it. That\'s why the two are <PulseWord>logically equivalent</PulseWord>: if one is true, the other is true, and you can use either. They\'re <PunchWord>interchangeable!</PunchWord></p>\n110:     </NoteSection>\n111:     <NoteSection title="Watch the lesson">\n112:       <ContrapositiveLecture/>\n113:     </NoteSection>\n114:     <NoteSection title="How to write it">\n115:       <p>Write the rule with an arrow: <strong>Light → Power</strong>. The sufficient condition goes on the left side of the arrow. The necessary condition goes on the right side of the arrow.</p>\n116:       <p>The contrapositive is <strong><Neg>Power</Neg> → <Neg>Light</Neg></strong>. A line through an idea means “not.” You get there in two steps:</p>\n117:       <ol className="ct-moves">\n118:         <li><strong>Step 1: Flip the sides.</strong> The right side goes on the left, and the left side goes on the right.</li>\n119:         <li><strong>Step 2: Negate both.</strong> Put “not” on each idea. If an idea already has a “not,” the two cancel: a double negative turns positive.</li>\n120:       </ol>\n121:       <FlipAndNegateDemo/>\n122:       <p>Same two steps when a side already has a “not.” Watch what happens to it:</p>\n123:       <DoubleNegativeDemo/>\n124:     </NoteSection>\n125:     <NoteSection title="What it is not">\n126:       <p>Two look-alikes fail. Wrong answer choices use them on purpose.</p>\n127:       <ThreeMoves/>\n128:     </NoteSection>\n129:     <NoteSection title="Read it, don\'t always write it">\n130:       <p>You can read a rule backward without writing anything: go from right to left and put a “not” on everything. Write the contrapositive when it helps you see the rule or connect it to another rule.</p>\n131:     </NoteSection>\n132:   </>;\n133: }\n134: \n135: function IndicatorNote({ group }: { group: \'group-1\' | \'group-2\' }) {\n136:   const [selected, setSelected] = useState(0);\n137:   const [opened, setOpened] = useState<number[]>([0]);\n138:   const [focusPanel, setFocusPanel] = useState(false);\n139:   const panels = useRef<(HTMLDivElement | null)[]>([]);\n140:   const id = useId();\n141:   const content = groupSections(group);\n142:   const expressions = content.slice(2, 9);\n143:   const names = expressions.map(section => section.props.title?.split(\':\')[0]);\n144:   useEffect(() => {\n145:     if (!focusPanel) return;\n146:     panels.current[selected]?.focus();\n147:     setFocusPanel(false);\n148:   }, [focusPanel, selected]);\n149:   const choose = (index: number, moveFocus = false) => {\n150:     setSelected(index);\n151:     setOpened(previous => previous.includes(index) ? previous : [...previous, index]);\n152:     setFocusPanel(moveFocus);\n153:   };\n154:   const last = selected === expressions.length - 1;\n155:   // Two introductions and the seven complete expression explanations belong\n156:   // together in one concept note, not nine separate navigation destinations.\n157:   return <>{content.slice(0, 2)}\n158:     <p className="co-word-guide">Work through all seven indicators. Each one does the same job in a different sentence, and each has its own trap to check. Use Next indicator to go in order, or choose any indicator to read it again.</p>\n159:     <div className="co-word-tabs" role="group" aria-label="Choose an indicator">{expressions.map((section, index) => <button type="button" key={section.props.title} aria-pressed={selected === index} aria-controls={`${id}-${index}`} onClick={() => choose(index)}>{names[index]}{opened.includes(index) && <><span aria-hidden="true"> ✓</span><span className="co-sr-only">, opened</span></>}</button>)}</div>\n160:     <p className="co-kicker" role="status">Indicator {selected + 1} of {expressions.length} · {opened.length} of {expressions.length} opened</p>230: \n231:     <ReferenceStep number={2} label="The sufficient condition" title="Sufficient means enough">\n232:       <div className="cr-sufficiency">\n233:         <p className="cr-sufficiency-lead"><strong>A sufficient condition is the “if” part of a rule. When it is true, the “then” part has to be true too.</strong></p>\n234:         <p>For example, in the rule below, being enrolled in law school is the <strong>sufficient condition</strong>. Being a student is the “then” part. It\'s what the rule guarantees.</p>\n235:         <blockquote><strong>If</strong> you\'re enrolled in law school, <strong>then</strong> you\'re a student.</blockquote>\n236:         <p>Someone might be enrolled. Someone might not. The rule doesn\'t say which. It only says what follows if they are.</p>\n237:         <p>Nothing else has to be true for the guarantee to hold. If someone is enrolled in law school, they have to be a student.</p>\n238:         <p>If they aren\'t enrolled, the rule says nothing. They might be a student or they might not.</p>\n239:         <p className="nc-takeaway"><strong>Being enrolled is enough. If it\'s true, being a student has to be true.</strong></p>\n240: \n241:         <section className="cr-sufficient-explanation sc-indicator-list" aria-label="Group 1 indicator words">\n242:           <h3>Group 1: Sufficient-condition indicators</h3>\n243:           <p>In our rule, <strong>if</strong> is the indicator and <strong>being enrolled in law school</strong> is the sufficient condition. These words and phrases point to it too:</p>\n244:           <ul>{sufficientFamily.map(entry => <li key={entry.word}>{entry.word}</li>)}</ul>\n245:           <p>The indicator is the word. The sufficient condition is the whole idea it points to.</p>\n246:         </section>\n247: \n248:         <SufficientConditionExperience/>\n249: \n250:         <SufficientConditionFilm key="sufficient-definition"/>\n251: \n252:         <section className="cr-sufficient-explanation cr-sufficient-checks" aria-label="Pop Quiz on sufficiency">\n253:           <div className="co-section-heading"><PopQuizTitle/></div>\n254:           <SufficientQuiz/>\n255:         </section>\n256:         <aside className="cr-sufficient-bridge"><h3>Next: The Necessary Condition</h3><p>Now look at the rule from the other side. Being a student is the <strong>necessary condition</strong>, the part that must be true. That\'s next.</p></aside>\n257:       </div>\n258:       <p>Next, we\'ll examine what the sufficient condition requires.</p>\n259:     </ReferenceStep>\n260: \n261:     <ReferenceStep number={3} label="The sufficient family" title="Different words can keep the same rule">\n262:       <p>An <strong>indicator</strong> is a word or phrase that helps you recognize a conditional rule. These indicators can introduce an idea that is enough to prove another.</p>\n263:       <p>For the next examples, use a different stated rule: anyone who borrows a locker has a key. We will express that same connection in several ways. We haven\'t said everyone with a key borrows a locker.</p>\n264:       <WordFamily entries={sufficientFamily} role="sufficient"/>\n265:       <p>When/whenever and any/anytime group related forms together. An ending changes the kind of case being discussed, but these examples keep the same connection. For example, every guest wearing a blue badge covers that whole group. Guest alone would include people without badges. Use the indicator to find the group, then keep the words that limit it.</p>\n266:       <ReferenceQuestion prompt="Every guest wearing a blue badge receives a lunch voucher. Which complete idea is enough?" options={[\'Guest\', \'Guest wearing a blue badge\', \'Receives a lunch voucher\']} correct={1} feedback={[\'Guest alone drops the badge restriction and would make a claim about unbadged guests.\', \'The rule gives a voucher to every guest wearing a blue badge. Keep that qualification with the person.\', \'The voucher is what the rule proves. It has not said every voucher recipient wears a blue badge.\']}/>\n267:       <details className="cr-extension"><summary>Two more Group 1 indicators</summary><p><strong>Where</strong> can state a rule across locations: “Where a collection point operates, parcels can be returned.” An operating collection point proves the availability of returns there. Availability is not proof that someone made a return.</p><p><strong>The only</strong> needs its complete phrase: “The only notebooks on this shelf are hardback.” A notebook on the shelf is hardback. The rule does not put every hardback notebook there.</p><p>These are part of your Group 1 work. Understand what the full phrase covers before using its group as a placement shortcut.</p></details>\n268:       <p>We have seen what can be enough. Now let\'s name what that condition requires.</p>\n269:     </ReferenceStep>\n270: \n271:     <ReferenceStep number={4} label="The necessary condition" title="Necessary means required">\n272:       <div className="cr-sufficiency nc-lesson">\n273:         <p className="cr-sufficiency-lead"><strong>A necessary condition is a requirement. It has to be met, but meeting it might not be enough.</strong></p>\n274:         <p>For example, you have to be a student to be enrolled in law school. That makes being a student <strong>necessary for law-school enrollment</strong>.</p>\n275:         <NecessaryConditionStatement/>\n276:         <p>But being a student doesn’t mean you’re enrolled in law school. You could be studying history instead.</p>\n277:         <p className="nc-takeaway"><strong>Being a student is required. It doesn’t prove enrollment on its own.</strong></p>\n278:         <section className="cr-sufficient-explanation nc-certainty" aria-label="Where the certainty is">\n279:           <h3>Where the certainty is</h3>\n280:           <p><strong>The necessary condition is what you can guarantee will occur if the sufficient condition happens.</strong></p>\n281:           <p>Look at the name. It\'s called necessary because it is necessary. If someone is enrolled in law school, being a student has to be true.</p>\n282:           <p>The sufficient condition opens the door. Once it\'s open, the necessary condition is guaranteed to be on the other side. That makes it <PulseWord>the place of certainty.</PulseWord> It\'s what you can prove.</p>\n283:         </section>\n284:         <NecessaryConditionExperience/>\n285:         <section className="cr-sufficient-explanation" aria-label="Why a requirement is not necessarily enough">\n286:           <h3>Two roles in the same rule</h3>\n287:           <p>Compare the two roles: <strong>being enrolled is enough to prove being a student; being a student is required for being enrolled.</strong> These describe the same rule from different sides.</p>\n288:           <p>Whenever the sufficient condition is true, the necessary condition must be true too. The rule does not tell us that either condition is actually true of a particular person.</p>\n289:           <details className="cr-extension"><summary>Can a condition be both necessary and sufficient?</summary><p>Yes, when the rule supports both directions. Necessary means required; it doesn\'t mean never enough. Our rule tells us that enrollment requires being a student. It doesn\'t say that being a student is enough to prove enrollment.</p></details>\n290:         </section>\n291:         <SufficientConditionFilm key="necessary-definition" assets={necessaryConditionFilms} title="Why the requirement is not enough here" ariaLabel="Short explanation of necessity"/>\n292:         <section className="cr-sufficient-explanation sc-indicator-list" aria-label="Group 2 indicator words">\n293:           <h3>Group 2: Necessary-condition indicators</h3>\n294:           <p>In an if-then rule, the necessary condition is the part after <strong>then</strong>. These words and phrases mark that same part, wherever it sits in the sentence. Each one tells you a <strong>necessary condition</strong> is in play. One evaluator declining to sign does not rule out another qualifying signer."\n\nsource-full-translation-03\nstatement: "Students exit the building when the final bell rings."\nindicators: [{"text":"when","group":1}]\nsufficient: "the final bell rings"\nnecessary: "students exit the building"\nifThen: "If the final bell rings, then students exit the building."\ncontrapositiveWords: "If students do not exit the building, then the final bell does not ring."\nexplanation: "The bell is sufficient under this rule. Exiting does not prove that the bell rang."\n\nsource-full-translation-06\nstatement: "Minutes are recorded whenever the committee meets."\nindicators: [{"text":"whenever","group":1}]\nsufficient: "the committee meets"\nnecessary: "minutes are recorded"\nifThen: "If the committee meets, then minutes are recorded."\ncontrapositiveWords: "If minutes are not recorded, then the committee does not meet."\nexplanation: "Meeting is sufficient even though it appears at the end of the rule."\n\nsource-full-translation-08\nstatement: "Expenditures can occur only if managers approve budgets first."\nindicators: [{"text":"only if","group":2}]\nsufficient: "expenditures can occur"\nnecessary: "managers have approved the budgets first"\nifThen: "If expenditures can occur, then managers have approved the budgets first."\ncontrapositiveWords: "If managers have not approved the budgets first, then expenditures cannot occur."\nexplanation: "Keep can occur and first. The rule is about authorization, not proof of spending."\n\nsource-full-translation-10\nstatement: "Access is granted only when user credentials are verified."\nindicators: [{"text":"only when","group":2}]\nsufficient: "access is granted"\nnecessary: "user credentials are verified"\nifThen: "If access is granted, then user credentials are verified."\ncontrapositiveWords: "If user credentials are not verified, then access is not granted."\nexplanation: "Access requires verified credentials. Verification alone does not guarantee access."\n\nsource-full-translation-11\nstatement: "Servers reboot only where network security protocols permit."\nindicators: [{"text":"only where","group":2}]\nsufficient: "servers reboot"\nnecessary: "network security protocols permit the reboot"\nifThen: "If servers reboot, then network security protocols permit the reboot."\ncontrapositiveWords: "If network security protocols do not permit the reboot, then servers do not reboot."\nexplanation: "Permission for this reboot is the requirement, not the mere existence of security protocols."\n\nsource-full-translation-13\nstatement: "Alarms won’t deactivate on their own if the emergency protocol fails to initiate within one minute."\nindicators: [{"text":"if","group":1}]\nsufficient: "the emergency protocol fails to initiate within one minute"\nnecessary: "alarms do not deactivate on their own"\nifThen: "If the emergency protocol fails to initiate within one minute, then alarms do not deactivate on their own."\ncontrapositiveWords: "If alarms deactivate on their own, then the emergency protocol initiates within one minute."\nexplanation: "Negate each complete condition. The contrapositive starts with alarms deactivating on their own and ends with protocol initiation within one minute."\n\nsource-full-translation-15\nstatement: "Reports are published whenever three reviewers call for revision."\nindicators: [{"text":"whenever","group":1}]\nsufficient: "three reviewers call for revision"\nnecessary: "reports are published"\nifThen: "If three reviewers call for revision, then reports are published."\ncontrapositiveWords: "If reports are not published, then it is not the case that three reviewers call for revision."\nexplanation: "Negating three reviewers call for revision does not mean three reviewers refuse. It denies the whole condition."\n\nsource-full-translation-19\nstatement: "When safety standards are unmet, inspections occur immediately."\nindicators: [{"text":"When","group":1}]\nsufficient: "safety standards are unmet"\nnecessary: "inspections occur immediately"\nifThen: "If safety standards are unmet, then inspections occur immediately."\ncontrapositiveWords: "If inspections do not occur immediately, then safety standards are met."\nexplanation: "Not immediately inspected is broader than never inspected. Timing belongs in the condition."\n\nsource-full-translation-20\nstatement: "Critical software patches are always applied before servers restart."\nindicators: [{"text":"always","group":2}]\nsufficient: "servers restart"\nnecessary: "critical software patches have been applied before the restart"\nifThen: "If servers restart, then critical software patches have been applied before the restart."\ncontrapositiveWords: "If critical software patches have not been applied before the restart, then servers do not restart."\nexplanation: "The patches must precede this restart. Later patching is not the required condition."\n\nsource-independent-07\nstatement: "The sun will rise, if you pay your taxes."\nindicators: [{"text":"if","group":1}]\nsufficient: "you pay your taxes"\nnecessary: "the sun rises"\nifThen: "If you pay your taxes, then the sun rises."\ncontrapositiveWords: "If the sun does not rise, then you do not pay your taxes."\nexplanation: "An arrow does not assert causation. Translate the given rule even when its subject matter is implausible."\n\nsource-independent-21\nstatement: "Food is in the fridge only when Rufus is not around."\nindicators: [{"text":"only when","group":2}]\nsufficient: "food is in the fridge"\nnecessary: "Rufus is not around"\nifThen: "If food is in the fridge, then Rufus is not around."\ncontrapositiveWords: "If Rufus is around, then food is not in the fridge."\nexplanation: "The contrapositive starts with Rufus being around and concludes that food is not in the fridge."\n\nsource-independent-23\nstatement: "I would not be able to see Arun if he were in the next room."\nindicators: [{"text":"if","group":1}]\nsufficient: "Arun is in the next room"\nnecessary: "I cannot see Arun"\nifThen: "If Arun is in the next room, then I cannot see Arun."\ncontrapositiveWords: "If I can see Arun, then Arun is not in the next room."\nexplanation: "Negating cannot see gives can see, not actually see. Keep ability consistent."\n\nsource-independent-24\nstatement: "Amar would not want to learn to ski if he were over 40."\nindicators: [{"text":"if","group":1}]\nsufficient: "Amar is over 40"\nnecessary: "Amar does not want to learn to ski"\nifThen: "If Amar is over 40, then Amar does not want to learn to ski."\ncontrapositiveWords: "If Amar wants to learn to ski, then Amar is not over 40."\nexplanation: "Negate the desire condition, not skiing itself. Over 40 is also different from 40 or older."\n\nsource-independent-34\nstatement: "Economic conditions improved only when the policy of free trade had been implemented."\nindicators: [{"text":"only when","group":2}]\nsufficient: "economic conditions improved"\nnecessary: "the policy of free trade had already been implemented"\nifThen: "If economic conditions improved, then the policy of free trade had already been implemented."\ncontrapositiveWords: "If the policy of free trade had not already been implemented, then economic conditions did not improve."\nexplanation: "Implementation precedes improvement. The conditional alone does not prove causation."'}]

}\n10: \n11:   private k(table: string, id: string): string {\n12:     return `${PREFIX}:${this.userId}:${table}:${id}`;\n13:   }\n14: \n15:   private prefix(table: string): string {\n16:     return `${PREFIX}:${this.userId}:${table}:`;\n17:   }\n18: \n19:   async get<T>(table: string, id: string): Promise<T | null> {\n20:     const raw = localStorage.getItem(this.k(table, id));\n21:     if (raw == null) return null;\n22:     try {\n23:       return JSON.parse(raw) as T;\n24:     } catch {\n25:       return null;\n26:     }\n27:   }\n28: \n29:   async set<T>(table: string, id: string, record: T): Promise<void> {\n30:     try {\n31:       localStorage.setItem(this.k(table, id), JSON.stringify(record));\n32:     } catch (err) {\n33:       console.warn('[LocalStoragePersistence] quota exceeded or write failed', err);\n34:       throw err;\n35:     }\n36:   }\n37: \n38:   async remove(table: string, id: string): Promise<void> {\n39:     localStorage.removeItem(this.k(table, id));\n40:   }\n41: \n42:   async list<T>(table: string, filter?: Partial<T>): Promise<T[]> {\n43:     const out: T[] = [];\n44:     const p = this.prefix(table);\n45:     for (let i = 0; i < localStorage.length; i++) {\n46:       const key = localStorage.key(i);\n47:       if (!key || !key.startsWith(p)) continue;\n48:       const raw = localStorage.getItem(key);\n49:       if (!raw) continue;\n50:       try {\n51:         const rec = JSON.parse(raw) as T;\n52:         if (!filter || matchesFilter(rec, filter)) out.push(rec);\n53:       } catch {\n54:         // skip malformed\n55:       }\n56:     }\n57:     return out;\n58:   }\n59: \n60:   // Blob storage is delegated to IndexedDBPersistence by V1Persistence;
